- 2026-03-10: Exact Algebra Core `R5` broadens bounded algebra solving to one-variable monomial/binomial rational factors and monomial/binomial radical radicands, while capping chained radical transforms at depth 2 and keeping all candidates subject to original-equation and preserved-constraint validation.
- 2026-03-10: The next preferred post-`R5` roadmap is settings + power/root/log expansion in this order: `SX1` settings foundation, `PRL1` display normalization, `PRL2` broad numeric support, `PRL3` bounded symbolic support, then `PRL4` bounded solve expansion.
- 2026-03-10: Settings is treated as a canonical app surface, not a `Menu` entry; visibility/readability controls and symbolic display preferences should live there as the app grows.
- 2026-03-10: `SX1` implements settings as a canonical shell-owned hybrid surface: docked right inspector at `>= 1180px`, right slide-over overlay below `1180px`, with a dedicated top-bar `Settings` entry point next to `Guide`.
- 2026-03-10: `SX1` keeps top-row quick toggles as shortcuts over the same persisted settings state and makes Settings/History mutually exclusive side surfaces.
- 2026-03-10: `SX1` persists `Display` and `Symbolic Display` preferences now, but broad symbolic output changes remain deferred to `PRL1`; in `SX1` those symbolic settings affect only the preview and saved preference state.
- 2026-04-03: `SX1.1` corrects the settings/history shell model: on wide windows both surfaces use a shared outboard right rail only when real spare gutter space exists, the calculator shell never shrinks to make room, and overlay remains the fallback when that space is unavailable.
- 2026-04-03: `PRL1` is display-only. Rendered exact math may be normalized through a bounded power/root/log display layer, but engine output, copy/editor flows, replay, and persisted history keep the raw exact LaTeX unchanged.
- 2026-04-03: `PRL1` makes `Symbolic Display` settings live across result rendering, with `Auto` power-leaning for awkward/nontrivial forms while still preserving familiar plain roots like `\sqrt{x}` and `\sqrt[3]{x}`.
- 2026-04-03: `PRL2` is numeric-only and real-domain-only: broad power/root/log evaluation ships first for `Calculate` and `Table`, while symbolic transforms and solve-family expansion stay deferred to `PRL3` and `PRL4`.
- 2026-04-03: Negative-base non-integer powers are accepted in `PRL2` only when the exponent is a true exact rational with odd denominator; decimal approximations that merely resemble those rationals are rejected.
- 2026-04-03: `Table` now keeps mixed-domain samples by marking invalid rows `undefined` and surfacing one table-level warning instead of failing the whole table.
- 2026-04-03: `PRL3` is symbolic normalization plus explicit transforms only; it may preprocess notational variants into already-supported Equation carriers, but it does not widen solve-family scope beyond the existing bounded backend.
- 2026-04-03: `PRL3` raw exact output is canonical power-leaning for awkward/nontrivial root-power forms, while simple `\sqrt{x}` and `\sqrt[3]{x}` remain roots; rendered style still layers through `PRL1` display preferences on top of that raw exact output.
- 2026-04-03: In `PRL3`, same-base log combine is automatic only under `Simplify`, while `Rewrite as Root`, `Rewrite as Power`, and `Change Base` are explicit algebra-tray transforms shared by `Calculate` and `Equation`.
- 2026-04-03: Symbolic substitution validation must preserve exact accepted branch LaTeX when a bounded solve produced it; post-validation rebuilds should not downgrade exact symbolic solutions into rounded decimal-only `exactLatex`.
- 2026-04-03: Repeated identical multiplicative factors in user-facing symbolic serializers must compact back into powers before emission (`xx -> x^2`, `4xx^3 -> 4x^4`); this is formatter debt, not a deferred `PRL4` solve-family concern.
- 2026-04-03: `PRL4` is Equation-first: broader log quotient/difference solving, bounded mixed-base normalization, and bounded radical/rational-power lifting ship only through `Equation > Symbolic`, while `Calculate` keeps its narrower PRL3 simplify contract for now.
- 2026-04-03: Recognized mixed-base log equations that leave bounded exact symbolic support should stop with explicit numeric guidance instead of silently falling through to numeric fallback or fake symbolic success.
- 2026-04-04: Numeric output is user-controlled through Settings with a typed `Approximate digits` input (`0..20`, default `6`), notation modes `decimal/scientific/auto`, and scientific styles `×10^n` or `e`; these controls are display-only and do not change solver effort or CE precision.
- 2026-04-04: Numeric output `Auto` notation switches to scientific form when `abs(x) >= 1e6` or `0 < abs(x) < 1e-4`; otherwise it stays in decimal form.
- 2026-04-04: Decimal-only symbolic Equation outcomes must not populate the exact line; symbolic provenance may remain, but decimal values belong only in approximate output.
- 2026-04-04: The next major Equation-solver roadmap candidate after `PRL4`/`NP1` is bounded composition and nested-function solving, starting with safe outer-function inversion plus range/image impossibility checks before any broader multi-layer search.
- 2026-04-04: Selected angle mode now applies to direct numeric trig input in `Calculate` even when the numeric argument contains `\pi`; `sin(\pi/2)` is treated as radians only in `RAD`, not silently preserved across `DEG` and `GRAD`.
- 2026-04-04: `COMP1` is Equation-first and one-layer only: Calcwiz now solves bounded `f(g(x))=c` families through safe outer inversion or image-aware trig reasoning with at most one guarded recursive handoff, and recognized trig compositions outside that bounded symbolic depth must stop with explicit numeric guidance instead of generic unsupported-family messaging.
- 2026-04-09: Calcwiz now treats `AGENTS.md` as the authoritative cross-agent workflow file, requires durable memory attribution through `.memory/PROTOCOL.md`, validates that protocol with `npm run test:memory-protocol`, and backfills historical ownership as `codex` with the user-approved model split at `2026-03-12`.
- 2026-04-04: `COMP2` keeps composition Equation-first and real-domain-only, but promotes nesting to an explicit policy: at most two successful outer inversions per solve attempt, followed by honest handoff into already-shipped bounded trig/PRL/algebra families or explicit numeric guidance when the remaining branch set is periodic, infinite, or outside supported exact scope.
- 2026-04-04: `COMP3` upgrades bounded trig follow-on from guidance-only to symbolic periodic families when the remaining carrier can still finish through bounded exact handoff; Equation now surfaces unit-aware family formulas, representative branches, and interval suggestions, while nonlinear-in-`k` carriers still stop honestly with structured periodic guidance.
- 2026-04-04: Explicit Equation numeric interval solving is now authoritative when a numeric interval is supplied: it runs before later symbolic-family stops, and all direct trig evaluation, residual checks, and candidate validation in that path must honor the selected angle unit (`rad`, `deg`, or `grad`).
- 2026-04-04: When Equation numeric interval solving misses a direct trig root and the solver can infer the sampled inner image, the failure message should explain that image and the current-unit branch family to target next instead of only suggesting wider intervals.
- 2026-04-04: Shared signed-number inputs should accept both plain decimals and scientific notation so large numeric-interval suggestions (for example `3e19`) are directly enterable across the app instead of being trapped behind giant expanded literals.
- 2026-04-05: `COMP4` extends composition solving in two bounded directions only: parameterized periodic follow-on may solve affine and pure power-form carriers `(ax+b)^n` for integer `n` from `2` to `6`, and inverse-trig outers (`arcsin/arccos/arctan`) may invert once and hand off once into already-supported guarded families; broader polynomial-in-`k` carriers and open-ended inverse-trig nesting remain deferred.
- 2026-04-05: The next planned composition milestone is `COMP5`, and it stays in the Equation-composition lane: it should deepen bounded periodic nesting and broaden bounded inverse-trig follow-on rather than folding calculus composition into the `COMP` sequence. Calculus composition is now treated as a separate future roadmap lane.
- 2026-04-05: `COMP5` stays Equation-first and still caps exact symbolic closure at finite branches or single-parameter periodic families; one additional structured periodic reduction is allowed beyond `COMP4`, but any path that would introduce a second independent periodic parameter must stop with structured guidance and suggested intervals instead of fake symbolic success.
- 2026-04-05: After `COMP5`, the composition roadmap is explicitly split into two named lanes instead of a vague future fork: `COMP6` continues bounded Equation composition (reciprocal trig, stronger periodic pruning, cleaner inverse/direct trig reductions), while calculus composition begins as `CALC-COMP1`/`CALC-COMP2`/`CALC-COMP3` for substitution antiderivatives, chain-rule structure, and composition-aware limits.
- 2026-04-05: `COMP6` stays bounded and Equation-only: reciprocal trig (`sec/csc/cot`) is implemented by rewrite into the existing `sin/cos/tan` composition machinery, while inverse/direct trig identities only collapse canonically when the inner carrier is provably inside the selected unit's principal range; anything beyond a single periodic parameter must stop with structured guidance instead of fake exact closure.
- 2026-04-07: `COMP7` keeps the Equation-composition lane periodic-heavy and honest: one additional structured periodic reduction is allowed through explicit `periodicReductionDepth`, discovered family trails must be preserved in the result surface, and any path that would require a second independent periodic parameter must stop with structured guidance instead of fake exact closure.
- 2026-04-07: Read-only math notation is now a persisted user setting (`rendered`, `plainText`, `latex`); `Copy Result` follows the visible notation, `To Editor` always uses canonical LaTeX, and the top-row `EXACT/DECIMAL/BOTH` control remains a synced shortcut over the canonical `outputStyle` setting.
- 2026-04-07: `COMP8` is a closure-quality Equation milestone, not a raw depth bump: bounded inverse/direct trig sawtooth closure is allowed only when the reduced carrier is affine (`x`, `ax+b`, or trivially sign-flipped/reordered affine equivalents), with at most one safe outer-inversion handoff and no expansion into nonlinear or multi-parameter sawtooth families.
- 2026-04-07: `COMP9` broadens sawtooth closure across the near-maximal current single-parameter carrier surface: after inverse/direct trig branch windows are generated, the solver may reuse bounded power, log, exponential, and selected root/rational-power carrier resolution, but it must still stop with structured guidance on general polynomial carriers, multi-parameter periodic families, or broader sawtooth normalization.
- 2026-04-07: `COMP10` extends the bounded single-parameter carrier resolver to normalized quadratics `ax^2+bx+c` and shifted powers `(ax+b)^n+c` for integer `n=2..4` across both plain periodic and sawtooth paths; broader polynomial solving, unrestricted sawtooth normalization, and multi-parameter periodic exact closure remain out of scope.
- 2026-04-07: The Rust numeric ODE backend now uses `mathexpr` instead of `meval`; maintainability and future Rust compatibility take priority over keeping the older parser, and the supported RHS surface remains the current frontend-emitted math subset with variables `x` and `y`.
- 2026-04-07: `POLY1` establishes `src/lib/algebra/polynomial-core.ts` as the canonical app-owned exact polynomial substrate for the bounded one-variable surface (degree `<= 4`, exact rational coefficients only); composition, factoring, and polynomial-pattern helpers should reuse that core instead of maintaining private exact polynomial parsers/arithmetic.
- 2026-04-08: `POLY2` uses one shared factor-first bounded exact engine for cubic/quartic polynomial support across guided `Equation > Polynomial`, free-form `Equation > Symbolic`, and `Calculate > Factor`; exact success is limited to rational-root, biquadratic, and bounded quadratic-pair families, while unsupported cubic/quartic families keep the existing numeric fallback or unsupported behavior instead of widening into general closed forms.
- 2026-04-08: Shared reusable math cores are now an explicit architecture principle for Calcwiz: when bounded algebra/solver logic is needed across multiple surfaces, we should centralize it in one maintained substrate instead of letting feature-local engines drift into duplicated logic. `POLY1`/`POLY2` are the first concrete application of that rule, and later algebra/radical/composition work should follow the same pattern.
- 2026-04-08: `RAD1` establishes `src/lib/algebra/radical-core.ts` as the canonical bounded radical-recognition substrate; symbolic radical normalization and guarded Equation algebra should share that one core for supported-radicand checks, condition synthesis, perfect-square radicand recognition, and bounded conjugate/rationalization eligibility instead of carrying duplicated radical-local logic.
- 2026-04-08: `RAD1` keeps Equation solve preprocess conservative even while simplify/factor normalization gets stronger: visible perfect-square square-root collapse into absolute-value form and bounded two-radical denominator rationalization are allowed on read-only normalization surfaces, but Equation solve must not auto-introduce new `|...|` carriers or widen radical-family solving in this milestone.
- 2026-04-08: `RAD2` widens radical Equation solving only through a bounded sequential pipeline: at most two radical-specific transform steps per solve path, bounded absolute-value branching only when produced by an exact supported radical step such as `\sqrt{(u)^2}`, and every surviving branch must still pass original-equation candidate validation plus merged domain/exclusion constraints.
- 2026-04-08: `POLY-RAD1` reuses one bounded algebraic follow-on bridge across both radical solve paths (`RAD2` sequential isolation and outer-inversion/composition handoff): exact algebraic-root follow-ons are limited to real one-variable biquadratic / quadratic-in-even-power families, `Factor` may surface the same algebraic quadratic factors, and `Simplify` may use the bridge only for direct radical normalization wins rather than general factor-style output.
- 2026-04-08: Calcwiz's architecture direction is now explicitly kernel-first: prefer a kernel-centered modular monolith with stable internal contracts, shared runtime/result/job/capability semantics, and clear pillar boundaries before attempting a true microkernel/plugin platform. External/plugin-style extensibility and Rust-authoritative runtime migration remain later phases, not immediate prerequisites.
- 2026-04-08: `ARCH1` is implemented as a narrow, parity-safe ownership-and-seams pass: runtime/kernel-adjacent types are split compatibility-first, kernel capability metadata stays internal and limited to six real execution seams, and app-shell execution now routes through typed Calculate/Equation controllers instead of growing new dependency-bag orchestration.
- 2026-04-08: `ARCH2` promotes `ARCH1` seams into static internal runtime hosts rather than inventing a broader platform: Equation stage order and Calculate action dispatch now live in minimal descriptor-backed hosts, kernel capabilities point to owning hosts, `table.build` stays metadata-linked only, and internal expression `solve` remains non-public.
- 2026-04-09: `ARCH3` keeps runtime envelopes and advisories intentionally narrow: `Calculate` and `Equation` now share one internal `DisplayOutcome` envelope helper, Equation numeric-solve controller gating is driven by tiny internal advisories instead of error-string matching, and those advisories must stay non-rendered and non-persisted.
- 2026-04-09: `ARCH4` adds a minimal shared runtime-policy layer for `Calculate`, `Equation`, and the planner boundary: `RuntimeAdvisories` now carries a small structured stop-reason taxonomy (`invalid-request`, `planner-hard-stop`, `range-guard`, `unsupported-family`) plus the existing tiny Equation numeric-solve advisory, all kept internal-only, non-rendered, and non-persisted.
- 2026-04-09: `ARCH5` adds one explicit internal default runtime execution profile for `Equation` and `Calculate`; Equation recursion/transform caps and Calculate numeric-fallback permissions are now owned by a shared kernel profile/budget layer, while planner behavior and all user-facing runtime controls remain unchanged.
- 2026-04-09: After `ARCH5`, the next algebra lane remains Poly-Rad, and `POLY-RAD2` is the next intended milestone. `POLY-RAD3` and any abs-bridge follow-up must be framed as bounded expansions on top of already-shipped `RAD2` behavior, not as if repeated-radical handling or radical-to-abs follow-on solving were starting from zero.
- 2026-04-09: `POLY-RAD2` is Equation-first and reuses one shared bounded polynomial-in-carrier bridge across both shipped radical entry paths. Supported carrier families are limited to affine, even-power affine, shifted even-power, and direct quadratic carriers; the bridge solves `P(u)=0` through the existing bounded polynomial engine, back-solves `u(x)=c` through shared carrier logic, then validates final `x` candidates against the original equation. No intentional new visible `Factor` / `Simplify` scope is included in this milestone.
- 2026-04-09: `POLY-RAD3` is solver-first and Equation-first: ordinary `RAD2` stays capped at two radical transform steps, while one dedicated repeated-clearing allowance may be spent only on recognized families that predictably land in existing bounded sinks. Visible non-Equation scope is limited to exact constant two-level square-root denesting in `Calculate > Simplify`.
- 2026-04-09: `POLY-RAD5` is a staged bounded transform-breadth milestone, not a new solver-depth or factorization milestone: Stage A widens shared square-root conjugate/rationalization coverage for stronger two-term families across Calculate and Equation, while Stage B is limited to selected three-term families that reduce to the Stage A surface after one canonical first multiply and at most one residual shared cleanup step.
- 2026-04-09: `POLY-RAD6` is the factor-first breadth milestone: Calcwiz recognizes one-pass mixed expressions that normalize to a bounded polynomial in a single supported carrier `u` (square roots and same-base sibling rational powers only), exposes the win primarily through `Calculate > Factor`, and allows Equation reuse only when the factor result deterministically improves an already-supported bounded sink.
- 2026-04-09: `ABS1` is the canonical repo milestone name for the roadmap's `ABS-BRIDGE1`.
- 2026-04-09: `ABS1` introduces one shared internal absolute-value core across direct abs equalities, transform-produced abs follow-ons, Calculate simplify normalization, and Equation numeric follow-up; visible exact solving remains bounded to deterministic branch families (`|u|=c`, `|u|=v`, `|u|=|v|`) and does not introduce a general-purpose piecewise engine or new visible `Factor` scope.
- 2026-04-10: `ABS2` broadens the abs lane around the same bounded `u = \pm v` branch model instead of adding case depth: direct affine-wrapped families (`a|u|+b=c`, `a|u|+b=v`, `a|u|+b=|v|`) and transform-produced wrapped closures are supported only when generated branches land in already-shipped bounded sinks, numeric follow-up becomes more branch-aware, `Calculate` stays Simplify-only, and nested abs towers, abs sums, inequalities, and general piecewise search remain out of scope.
- 2026-04-10: `ABS3` keeps the shared absolute-value branch model fixed at `u = \pm v` and broadens exact closure by admitting stronger bounded carrier families only when every branch lands in already-shipped sinks. Stronger unresolved families now surface stronger-carrier guidance, and transformed branch solving must not leak non-real direct-symbolic branch roots into merged exact real-output lines.
- 2026-04-10: `ABS4` stays in the abs lane and broadens outer algebra around one abs target instead of introducing `BRANCH1`: bounded one-placeholder outer-polynomial closure `P(|u|)=0` is allowed on the existing exact polynomial surface, trig/composition carriers are included only as reuse of already-shipped sinks after the same `u = \pm v` split, and any branch that reaches only guided periodic/composition output keeps the overall abs family unresolved rather than partially exact.
- 2026-04-10: `COMP11` is a deliberately broad composition milestone: exact composition closure now extends to reduced polynomial carriers on the existing bounded one-variable polynomial surface, shared Equation composition/periodic budgets are raised to a bounded depth of `3`, and selected exact two-parameter periodic families are allowed only when the first reduction yields exact numeric branch constants and the remaining continuation is direct trig on affine `x`.
- 2026-04-10: Large math-lane milestones should default to a two-slice structure when practical: slice `A` ships the real capability expansion, and slice `B` follows immediately as the polish/trust/readback/guidance pass for the same lane. This is now the preferred pattern for heavier roadmap steps such as `COMP12A`/`COMP12B` and later `ABS5A`/`ABS5B`.
- 2026-04-10: `COMP12A` broadens the composition lane by carrier breadth, not by adding more periodic parameters: reduced-carrier periodic and sawtooth exact success is now allowed for already-shipped bounded single-family carriers beyond reduced polynomials, explicit `x` closure remains preferred whenever it already exists, and mixed poly-rad carriers stay out of scope for this slice.
- 2026-04-10: `COMP12B` is polish-only for the `COMP12A` surface: admitted reduced-carrier periodic and sawtooth exact outcomes should render as intentional exact successes with canonical reduced-carrier summaries, guided composition stops should explain their specific blocker instead of reusing generic unsupported wording, exact reduced-carrier context should render separately from stop-reason messaging in the result card, and numeric follow-up may gain composition-aware guidance wording but not a new solving algorithm or broader exact surface.
- 2026-04-10: `ABS5A` makes `t = |u|` a first-class bounded placeholder across shipped non-periodic outer sinks: the abs lane may now reuse bounded logarithmic, exponential/same-base, radical/root, and rational-power follow-ons over one exact abs target, allow one additional non-periodic outer layer before returning to the existing abs sink path, keep the same `u = \pm c` branch model, and leave periodic outer families composition-owned.
- 2026-04-11: `ABS5B` keeps abs polish on the existing generic `DisplayOutcome.detailSections` surface instead of adding an abs-specific result card; `solveSummaryText` stays short and outcome-level, while reduction context, generated branches, and exact-closure boundaries render as separate detail sections.
- 2026-04-10: Architecture direction for future algebra growth is now explicit: keep `src/lib/kernel/*` as the single runtime kernel, continue extracting reusable bounded algebra cores when needed, and avoid introducing multiple per-engine microkernels for piecewise/case handling, transforms, or other math families unless a later real runtime/plugin boundary appears. If architecture resumes in this lane, the preferred next extractions are shared `branch-core` / `case-core` and `transform-core`, with a thin algebra registry only as a later optional coordination layer.
- 2026-04-10: Commit metadata recording is now part of the same intended checkpoint: when a task is committed, session `commit-log.md` and related `commit_hash` fields should be updated within that same commit flow whenever possible, rather than creating a second metadata-only commit afterward.
- 2026-04-10: Transform handling and piecewise/case handling should not be merged into one new subsystem. If Calcwiz extracts more algebra cores here, they should stay separate: `transform-core` owns deterministic transform recognition, exact application, preserved-constraint inputs, and sink-prediction hints; `branch-core` owns bounded branch generation, branch constraints, and branch metadata for abs/principal-range/periodic-style families. Use `branch-core` rather than `piecewise-core` unless the repo later commits to broader piecewise algebra. Default architecture preference is `transform-core` first because it is lower-risk, unless the active roadmap becomes branch-heavy enough to justify `branch-core` first.
- 2026-04-10: `ARCH6A` is a pure extraction milestone: `src/lib/algebra/transform-core.ts` now owns deterministic transform descriptors, exact transform application, eligibility checks, normalized output comparison, and shared expression/equation transform mechanics, while `src/lib/algebra/algebra-transform.ts` stays the stable public facade and no visible transform behavior changes in this pass.
- 2026-04-10: `ARCH6B` is a pure extraction milestone: `src/lib/algebra/branch-core.ts` now owns normalized branch sets, branch-constraint merging, branch-pair adapters, and periodic/principal-range metadata merge behavior for the Equation/abs/rewrite/substitution lane, while public result-facing types stay unchanged and no visible solver/UI behavior changes in this pass.
- 2026-04-11: Calcwiz's future Playground should be treated as a level-based incubation system, not as a production core or hidden solver stack. Stable app code must never import from Playground code, and any successful experiment must graduate into stable architecture by extraction or rewrite rather than by direct reuse.
- 2026-04-11: `PGL1` establishes the first implementation boundary for Playground without turning it into a platform: keep Playground as a top-level tree outside `src/`, enforce the one-way dependency law only for stable TypeScript product code in `src/`, and include only thin starter records/templates rather than schema validation, workflow automation, or experiment-execution infrastructure.
- 2026-04-11: `PGL2` makes Playground records canonical without adding platform machinery: Markdown records are authoritative, YAML manifests are tiny companion summaries only, every real experiment should have a record + manifest + index entry, and the first seeded starter experiment is `sym-search-planner-ordering` at `level-0-research` in the symbolic-search lane.
- 2026-04-11: `PGL3` symbolic-search experiments use a dedicated `test:playground` Vitest harness plus an export-only guarded-stage replay seam; the stable guarded solver default order remains unchanged.
- 2026-04-11: The first `sym-search-planner-ordering` PGL3 run stays at `level-0-research`: both alternate whole-stage orderings produced no exact improvements and each introduced the same honesty regression on the guided abs/composition boundary case `2^{|sin(x^5+x)|}=2^{1/2}`.
- 2026-04-14: Calculator-visible Playground work is now separated from the core incubation ladder as a follow-on roadmap family named `PGL-VIS`; it begins only after the main `PGL` roadmap is sufficiently complete and should start with an internal developer/operator console rather than a normal-user-facing Playground mode.
- 2026-04-14: `PGL4` is now explicitly an external-compute foundations milestone rather than a first provider pilot: it adds provider-neutral runner/job/artifact contracts, checked-in JSON templates, ignored local SSH-shaped profiles, and a local harness over one real existing Playground workload while keeping real SSH/provider execution deferred.
- 2026-04-14: Future external-compute config should use checked-in template JSON plus ignored repo-local `*.local.json` profiles. SSH is the first expected future transport, but `PGL4` keeps SSH structurally validated and intentionally non-executable.
- 2026-04-14: `PGL5` is a singular VM-first external-compute milestone, not a `PGL5A/PGL5B` split. The first real SSH target is the user-owned `<user-ssh-target>` VM, while provider/rented-host execution remains a later sequencing decision.
- 2026-04-14: `PGL5` turns the `PGL4` SSH contract into one bounded remote pilot only: upload JSON inputs, run one dedicated remote Playground entrypoint for `sym-search-planner-ordering`, pull back the manifest and summary, and require a local parity report before calling the pilot successful.
- 2026-04-14: `PGL5+` is the explicit pre-adoption hardening gate between `PGL5` and any later provider-host or `PGL6` discussion. The canonical operator path is now `npm run playground:ssh-vm -- --profile <path> --job <path>`, and the gate requires preflight checks, classified failure modes, provenance, and one live success path plus induced failure-path proofs before any adoption review.
- 2026-04-24: Version 1 platform direction is now Linux-first, superseding the earlier Windows-first v1 target while preserving cross-platform ground for Windows/macOS through Tauri, TypeScript, Rust, and validation gates.
- 2026-04-24: External compute remains a valid Playground lane but is postponed after `PGL5+`; the VM-first SSH proof is preserved, provider-host expansion is deferred, and the next incubation work should improve the Playground system and non-remote experiment discipline until the core calculator and solver roadmap are more stable.
- 2026-04-24: Near-term product sequencing now pauses broad algebra breadth and moves to a calculus roadmap: first `CALC-AUDIT0` verifies current calculus and Advanced Calc status plus safe algebra-core reuse, then bounded calculus-composition milestones should proceed through `CALC-CORE1`, `CALC-COMP1`, `CALC-COMP2`, `CALC-LIM1`, `CALC-INT1`, and `CALC-POLISH1` unless the audit changes the order.
- 2026-04-24: `CALC-AUDIT0` locks `CALC-CORE1` before `CALC-COMP1`. The audit found enough duplication in basic Calculus vs Advanced Calc result origins, warning policy, and numeric fallback handling that a narrow shared calculus evaluation/reporting boundary should land before new substitution-antiderivative or composition capability work.
- 2026-04-24: After `CALC-CORE1`, `CALC-*` milestones are dependency-gated product-calculus milestones. Each one must state the algebra, differentiation, domain, formatting, and result-surface substrates it needs; if a needed substrate is missing or unstable, calculus work pauses and the prerequisite is planned explicitly instead of adding calculus-local algebra workarounds.
- 2026-04-24: `CALC-CORE2` permits `CALC-COMP1` to proceed only as a narrow derivative-backed substitution/composition milestone. Broader integration-by-parts hardening, partial fractions, rational integration, radical/rationalizing substitutions, and definite-integral trust remain deferred or blocked until their algebra/domain prerequisites are explicit.
- 2026-04-25: `CALC-CORE3` locks Basic Calculus and Advanced Calc indefinite integrals to one shared app-owned symbolic backend for all shared behavior. Separate UI sections and Advanced-only workflows may remain, but private Basic-vs-Advanced symbolic integral rule stacks are disallowed unless a future milestone documents a real product boundary.
- 2026-04-25: `CALC-COMP1` exposes integration strategy badges for symbolic indefinite-integral wins while keeping antiderivative verification status internal. `ResultOrigin` values remain stable; visible badges describe strategy, not proof strength.
- 2026-04-25: `CALC-DIFF1` exposes derivative strategy badges for supported derivative wins while keeping proof/check status and step-by-step derivations internal. Known inverse trig and parser-recognized inverse hyperbolic families are supported; arbitrary generic inverse-function theorem handling for `f^{-1}` remains deferred, and explicit reciprocals such as `(sin(x))^{-1}` must stay reciprocal expressions.
- 2026-04-25: The original `CALC-COMP2` derivative-structure milestone is now parked as mostly absorbed by `CALC-DIFF1`. The next active calculus milestone should be `CALC-LIM1` unless a specific derivative readback/domain blocker is found first.
- 2026-04-25: `CALC-LIM1` strengthens finite limits through bounded known-form and removable-hole rules only. It does not introduce a general asymptotic engine, multivariable limits, new `ResultOrigin` values, or visible limit strategy badges; numeric fallback must remain visibly labeled.
- 2026-04-25: `CALC-LIM2` intentionally treats trustworthy signed infinities as successful finite-limit results when direction/sign evidence is clear, while preserving mismatch/domain stops for unsafe cases. Typed directional targets such as `0^+` and `0^-` are normalized at shared input boundaries, and rational-hole expansion remains limited to existing rational/factor/cancel substrates without adding a general asymptotic or series engine.
- 2026-04-25: Tracked durable memory is public-promotable by default: exact local filesystem prefixes, private operator names, and local SSH target aliases should be represented with placeholders such as `<local-source>`, `<local-repo-path>`, and `<user-ssh-target>`. Exact environment mappings may be kept only in ignored scratchpads or local config, not in tracked memory.
- 2026-04-25: `CALC-LIM3` may combine bounded local-order reasoning, existing rational normalization/cancellation, and listed elementary equivalents into rule-based symbolic limit wins, but it must not become a general series/asymptotic engine. User-facing limit explanation should use accurate `DisplayOutcome.detailSections` notes rather than new limit strategy badges, and `ResultOrigin` values remain stable.
- 2026-04-26: `domain-range-CORE1` is a shared bounded real-domain/range substrate before `CALC-INT1`, not a calculus-local workaround and not an expansion of `branch-core`. It may collect common real-domain constraints, prove simple ranges, and provide point/one-sided/interval safety checks, but full inequality solving, broad domain display, piecewise engines, and definite-integral trust changes remain out of scope for this milestone.
- 2026-04-26: `CALC-INT1` may use verified antiderivatives for exact finite definite integrals only after bounded interval-safety checks find no concrete real-domain violation. Detected unsafe intervals must stop before numeric fallback; safe unsupported finite definite integrals may still use clearly labeled adaptive Simpson fallback. Definite-integral UX uses existing detail sections for method and safety notes, while broad exact improper integration and new definite-integral strategy badges remain deferred.
- 2026-04-26: `REL0` establishes public-repo guardrails before broader promotion: `main` should require PR review, Code Owner review, conversation resolution, no force pushes/deletions, restricted direct pushes, and status check `ci-linux`. Linux preview releases are manual/tag-triggered only, draft/prerelease by default, and must not include Playground/external compute, signing keys, private paths, provider-host compute, or claims of full CAS parity.
- 2026-04-27: The next clean-base sequence should finish public-release foundations before new math breadth: `REL1`, `PILLARS0`, and `MATH-GOLDEN0` are preferred before returning to `CALC-POLISH1` unless public usage exposes an urgent calculus UX blocker. `FRICAS-CTX0` is allowed only as an isolated context-mirror research lane after a further incubation-system strengthening pass: FriCAS is context, not authority; Calcwiz must not depend on it, submodule it, copy code by default, or inherit its identity, and any useful idea must be translated into a Calcwiz-native bounded form through Playground/incubation before stable adoption.
- 2026-04-28: `.memory/sources/` is the canonical location for preserving external roadmaps, research files, and exported discussion sources as verbatim snapshots. Snapshot files should not be wrapped or edited; capture metadata, hashes, original placeholder paths, and downstream interpretation links belong in `.memory/sources/INDEX.md`. `.memory/research/references/sources.md` remains a legacy/reference note rather than the as-is preservation location.
- 2026-04-28: `REL1` preview packaging should run a fuller release gate before Tauri bundle creation: launch preflight, memory protocol, unit tests, UI tests, lint, frontend build, Rust `cargo check`, and `tauri:build`. First Linux preview releases remain draft/prerelease by default and must not imply production stability, full CAS parity, external compute, auto-updating, signing, or Windows/macOS artifact guarantees.
- 2026-04-28: `PILLARS0` establishes eight small public project pillars as governance docs guarded by `npm run test:pillars`, not as a runtime architecture layer. `MATH-GOLDEN0` remains the next correctness milestone; telemetry, crash uploaders, config migrations, plugin APIs, FriCAS research, incubation changes, and solver/math behavior changes remain out of scope for `PILLARS0`.
- 2026-04-28: `MATH-GOLDEN0` protects shipped behavior with a small typed golden corpus over stable mode boundaries, not UI snapshots or aspirational examples. Golden cases should prefer stable substrings, origins, strategies, warnings, detail titles, and controlled-stop metadata; broad benchmark corpora and future challenge sets belong outside this gate.
- 2026-04-28: `CALC-POLISH1` makes typed calculus replay context the durable history policy for new guided Calculus and Advanced Calc entries. The fields remain optional, old history entries must keep replaying through LaTeX inference fallback, and replay metadata may preserve UI/tool state but must not imply new math capability, new `ResultOrigin` values, or new strategy labels.
- 2026-04-30: `INCUBATION-LABS0` establishes the only allowed first calculator-visible incubation surface as a generated, read-only Labs catalog: tooling may read Playground manifests/records at generation time, stable app runtime may import only the committed `src/lib/labs` snapshot, `playground/...` paths are inert display text, and the visible Labs mode is developer-only behind `VITE_SHOW_LABS=1` with no experiment execution, remote controls, result delegation, or history mixing.
- 2026-05-01: `INCUBATION-SOURCES0` establishes `playground/sources/` as the controlled registry for external CAS/math source mirrors. Cloned repos must live only under ignored `playground/sources/mirrors/<mirror-id>/`, committed metadata is context-only, stable `src/` code must not import or read source mirrors, and FriCAS/SymPy/Maxima/SageMath/Giac-XCAS/SymEngine may inform Calcwiz only through translated Playground/incubation work, not direct dependency, submodule, code copying, or identity inheritance.
- 2026-05-01: `FRICAS-CTX0` makes FriCAS an active context mirror only, not an authority or backend. Its outputs are research notes, a Playground `source-context` record, and a typed challenge/reference corpus; future use must choose bounded Calcwiz-native prototypes from the idea ledger, and the corpus is evidence for research, not a product parity contract.
- 2026-05-01: The post-`FRICAS-CTX0` native roadmap starts with `ALG-CAPS0` capability facts before heavier polynomial, integration, series, Grobner, or matrix work. FriCAS-derived work must first establish shared readiness language so later milestones do not invent private local capability checks.
- 2026-05-01: Post-FriCAS work follows a core-first rule: strengthen existing Calcwiz-owned cores before jumping into advanced fields, unless a concrete blocker proves the advanced field is required. Grobner, regular chains, broad asymptotics, and full Risch-style integration remain Playground-only or deferred until current algebra/domain/calculus substrates are explicitly ready.
- 2026-05-01: Current Matrix and Vector support is treated as numeric product workspaces plus notation pads, not reusable algebra cores. Exact linear algebra work such as `MATRIX-EXACT0` is postponed behind `VEC-MAT-AUDIT0` and likely a small `VEC-MAT-CORE0` boundary so future determinant/rank/echelon/inverse/system work has explicit coefficient-domain and result-envelope ownership.
- 2026-05-20: `VEC-MAT-AUDIT0` confirms Matrix and Vector are current numeric product workspaces with notation helpers, not shared symbolic/exact algebra substrates. Future exact linear algebra must start with `VEC-MAT-CORE0` ownership over exact scalar support, vector/matrix data models, coefficient-domain gates, and result envelopes before `MATRIX-EXACT0` can reopen.
- 2026-05-20: `ALG-CAPS0` establishes a code-backed math-substrate readiness table in `src/lib/algebra/capability-readiness.ts`, separate from runtime execution capabilities in `src/lib/kernel/capabilities.ts`. The post-FriCAS sequence is now `ALG-CAPS0 -> VEC-MAT-CORE0 -> POLY-CORE-AUDIT1 -> INT-CANDIDATE2`; vector/matrix core is `blocked` until `VEC-MAT-CORE0`, and exact linear algebra remains `defer`.
- 2026-05-20: `VEC-MAT-CORE0` creates two sibling reusable numeric cores, `matrix-core` and `vector-core`, rather than one fused linear-algebra engine. Product Matrix/Vector adapters must preserve shipped numeric behavior and UI result wording; exact linear algebra remains deferred until exact scalar readiness and coefficient-domain gates are explicit. The next native milestone is `POLY-CORE-AUDIT1`.
- 2026-05-20: `POLY-CORE-AUDIT1` confirms `polynomial-core` is `ready-with-adapter`, not a broad polynomial algebra engine. Shipped support covers bounded one-variable exact rational polynomial parsing/arithmetic/readback and current factor/rational/numeric-root/solve consumers; polynomial gcd/division, square-free factorization, resultants, partial fractions, Grobner/elimination, broad rational integration, and exact matrix algebra remain blocked or deferred. The next native milestone is `INT-CANDIDATE2`.
- 2026-05-20: `POLY-RAT-CORE0` promotes exact polynomial division/GCD, primitive normalization, rational-function normalization, and bounded distinct-linear partial-fraction readiness into shared internal substrates. This unlocks planning for bounded `INT-RAT1`, but rational integration is not adopted yet; repeated factors, irreducible quadratic decomposition, square-free factorization, resultants, Grobner/elimination, and exact matrix algebra remain blocked or deferred.
- 2026-05-20: `INT-CANDIDATE2` adds internal integration candidate metadata without changing visible integration behavior. Integration attempts may now record method, prerequisites, blocked prerequisites, verification status, controlled failure class, readiness notes, and domain hazards; rational integration gaps must remain blocked on polynomial gcd/division/partial-fraction readiness rather than becoming calculus-local workarounds.
- 2026-05-20: `APPMAIN-SLIM0` locks the near-term app-shell organization rule: `src/AppMain.tsx` remains the orchestration root for global state, refs, routing, history, keyboard handling, execution handlers, and shell ownership, while `src/app/workspaces/*Workspace.tsx` components own render/view boundaries. Further state extraction is deferred to a later `APPMAIN-SLIM1`-style milestone rather than bundled into this no-behavior refactor.
- 2026-05-21: `MEMORY-ORG0` makes calendarized durable-memory layout canonical for journals and sessions. New journals must use `.memory/journal/YYYY-MM/YYYY-MM-DD.md`, new session dossiers must use `.memory/sessions/YYYY-MM/YYYY-MM-DD/YYYY-MM-DD__slug/`, and the memory protocol validator should reject deprecated flat entries.
- 2026-05-21: `MEMORY-ORG1` makes the typed `.memory/research/` taxonomy canonical. New interpreted research artifacts should go under `roadmaps/`, `checklists/YYYY-MM/YYYY-MM-DD/`, `readiness/`, `audits/`, `source-context/<mirror>/`, `architecture/`, or `references/`; root-level research artifacts are disallowed except `README.md` and `INDEX.md`.
- 2026-06-04: Manual verification checklists now use day-level calendarization under `.memory/research/checklists/YYYY-MM/YYYY-MM-DD/`. Month-level checklist files are considered clutter and are rejected by `npm run test:memory-protocol`.
- 2026-05-21: `LIB-ORG0` locks the `src/lib` organization pass to clean import rewrites with no root-level compatibility shims. Each `LIB-ORG` phase must commit separately after verification and record what moved in durable memory.
- 2026-05-21: Journal months with completed milestone work must have corresponding session-month coverage under `.memory/sessions/YYYY-MM/`. Closely related slices may share one dossier to keep memory navigable, but the dossier must point back to the relevant journal, checklist, research, and commit evidence.
- 2026-05-21: `PGL-VIS1` allows interactive Labs execution only through an explicitly enabled dev-server runner bridge. Stable `src` runtime code must still not import Playground code, approved runners must declare supported input kinds, outputs must be labeled experimental/developer-only, release builds must not expose runner execution, and Labs runs must not enter normal calculator history or stable result provenance.
- 2026-05-21: `PGL-VIS1-POLISH` makes Labs mode own the top display as a live developer-only preview surface. Labs should suppress stale normal calculator outcomes, render comparison math through stable display components, and keep raw LaTeX in details/accessibility surfaces rather than the primary visual table.
- 2026-05-21: Incubation Infrastructure is now the preferred framing for the research-to-product pipeline. The next infrastructure milestone should formalize source-mirror security, Labs runner policy, area-study synthesis modes, missing-capability gates, and truth-first math output taxonomy before starting a new major cross-engine area study.
- 2026-05-21: `FRICAS-CTX0` is closed as a single-source context milestone. Future work should not be organized around FriCAS alone; ideas first noticed through FriCAS should become multi-source `AREA-*` capability studies where FriCAS is one evidence source alongside other relevant systems.
- 2026-05-21: `INCUBATION-INFRA1` makes source mirrors, Labs runners, and area studies explicit guardrails before new cross-engine research. Source mirrors have security tiers and no-execution policies; Labs runners are dev-only, no-history, no-remote, and no-source-mirror-execution; area studies are the required structure for future multi-source capability synthesis.
- 2026-05-21: `SOURCE-CAPTURE1` uses shallow static captures as the default for local source mirrors. Captured mirrors remain context-only under ignored `playground/sources/mirrors/<mirror-id>/`, use `static-only` plus `no-execute`, and may be deepened later only when a concrete area study needs history. Closed/proprietary calculator installations are excluded from the source-mirror registry.
- 2026-05-21: `AREA-POLY-RAT0` establishes the first multi-source capability-area study over polynomial and rational substrates. Its exact next recommendation is bounded `INT-RAT1`, limited to one-variable exact rational functions with distinct rational linear partial fractions and derivative-backed verification. Repeated factors, irreducible quadratics, square-free factorization, resultants, Grobner/elimination, exact linear algebra, source execution, and code copying remain out of scope or deferred.
- 2026-05-21: `INT-RAT1` adopts only the distinct-rational-linear partial-fraction slice into stable calculus. The `partial-fractions` strategy is allowed for verified app-owned one-variable exact rational integrations over shared `POLY-RAT-CORE0` substrates; repeated factors, irreducible quadratic partial fractions, square-free factorization, resultants, Grobner/elimination, broad rational integration, source-mirror execution, and Risch/Liouville-style work remain deferred.
- 2026-05-22: `AREA-POLY-RAT1` makes full-domain polynomial/rational atlas work the planning source for future POLY/RAT milestones. The exact next recommendation is `POLY-RAT-CORE1`, not `INT-RAT2`: repeated linear factors, irreducible quadratic readiness, square-free/factor-multiplicity facts, and stronger rational stop metadata should be added to the algebra substrate before calculus widens again. Future polynomial/rational implementation slices should come from the multi-source atlas, not single-engine imitation.
- 2026-05-22: `.memory/research/roadmaps/poly-rat-native-roadmap.md` is the planning bridge from `AREA-POLY-RAT1` to implementation. `POLY-RAT-CORE1` should be a substrate leap, `INT-RAT2` should be a later consumer, and simplification or elimination work should reopen through separate `AREA-SIMPLIFY0` / `AREA-POLY-ELIM0` studies rather than being smuggled into rational integration.
- 2026-05-22: `POLY-RAT-CORE1` promotes repeated-rational-linear and irreducible-quadratic denominator family readiness into the shared rational substrate without widening calculus. `INT-RAT1` distinct-linear behavior remains stable; `INT-RAT2` may later consume the new typed readiness envelopes only through derivative-backed verification and existing domain-safety gates.
- 2026-05-22: `AREA-SIMPLIFY0` chooses `SIMPLIFY-CORE0` before `INT-RAT2`. The blocker is broader than rational-integration result wording: Calcwiz needs a bounded shared normal-form/readback/equivalence policy that preserves denominator/domain facts before visible rational-calculus widening resumes. This is not approval for a broad simplifier or single-engine imitation.
- 2026-05-22: `SIMPLIFY-CORE0` is an internal policy substrate only. It defines form intent, equivalence trust, and preserved facts for readback/adoption gates, but it does not add new simplification rules, visible result labels, UI badges, solver behavior, or calculus behavior by itself.
- 2026-05-22: `INT-RAT2` may widen the existing `partial-fractions` integration strategy to bounded repeated rational linear and irreducible quadratic denominator families only when the candidate antiderivative passes the shared verification/readback policy. It must preserve `inverse-trig` and `derivative-ratio` priority, add no new `ResultOrigin` values or strategy labels, and keep broad rational integration, Risch/Liouville work, high-degree/algebraic-root factor families, and source-mirror execution out of scope.
- 2026-05-22: `CALC-RAT-READBACK0` treats rational integral display/readback as a separate polish layer from integration-family adoption. It may clean app-owned `partial-fractions` LaTeX, add explanatory detail sections, and update Guide examples, but it must not add new calculus capability, `ResultOrigin` values, strategy labels, source-mirror execution, or Playground runner behavior.
- 2026-05-22: `AREA-ASSUMPTIONS0` selects `ASSUMPTIONS-CORE0` as the next bounded internal substrate. The need is broader than denominator exclusions or branch policy alone: Calcwiz needs request-scoped, result-attached facts for domain constraints, exclusions, branch/principal-range choices, interval hazards, candidate rejection, and equivalence trust before more algebra/calculus/table/graphing-readiness widening. This is not approval for global mutable assumptions, broad inequality solving, graphing behavior changes, or source-mirror execution.
- 2026-05-22: `ASSUMPTIONS-CORE0` defines assumptions as scoped internal facts, not global runtime assumptions. The bounded vocabulary covers domain exclusions, domain constraints, interval hazards, branch/principal-range facts, candidate rejection, and equivalence trust, with source and trust metadata. It does not add a public `assume(...)` feature, broad inequality solving, graphing behavior, branch-cut theorem proving, or visible result changes.
- 2026-05-22: `ASSUMPTIONS-ADOPT1` adopts assumption facts internally only. Existing rational-function, domain-range, branch, candidate-rejection, and simplify/readback facts may map into `AssumptionFact[]`, but visible result text, detail sections, badges, result origins, history/provenance, source-mirror boundaries, and Labs runner behavior must remain unchanged until a later explicit readback or product milestone.
- 2026-05-22: `ASSUMPTIONS-READBACK0` is the explicit visible-readback milestone for the scoped assumption fact spine. It may add explanatory `DisplayOutcome.detailSections` for existing domain exclusions, interval hazards, candidate checking, branch facts, and trust notes, but it must not change primary answer LaTeX, solver/calculus/table behavior, strategy labels, result origins, history/provenance, source-mirror boundaries, or Labs runner behavior.
- 2026-05-22: `ASSUMPTIONS-POLISH1` makes concise fact readback the default product posture. Full source/trust/check detail remains available through the opt-in `Detailed Facts` setting, but backend fact generation and primary answers remain unchanged.
- 2026-05-22: `DOMAIN-GRAPH-READY0` creates shared domain sampling readiness for Table and future graphing work. It may produce reusable sampled-hazard and assumption facts, but it is not plotting, graph UI, interval arithmetic, or a graph correctness proof.
- 2026-05-23: Future milestone numbering should reserve `0` for audit, study, surveillance, and readiness work. Actual implementation work should start at `1` unless an older historical milestone name already exists; for example, the study is `AREA-POLY-ELIM0` and the first implementation slice would be `POLY-ELIM1`.
- 2026-05-23: Graphing remains an endgame product area, not a near-term recommendation. Existing graph-readiness infrastructure such as `DOMAIN-GRAPH-READY0` remains historical and reusable, but future planning should not propose graphing milestones again until the calculator is much closer to broadly stabilized/completed.
- 2026-05-23: `AREA-POLY-ELIM0` selects `AREA-EXACT-LINEAR-ALGEBRA0` as the exact next move before `POLY-ELIM1`. The study found that resultants, Grobner bases, and elimination require exact coefficient-domain and exact linear-algebra readiness; Calcwiz should not invent exact row-reduction or matrix algebra locally inside polynomial-elimination code.
- 2026-05-23: `AREA-EXACT-LINEAR-ALGEBRA0` selects `EXACT-LINEAR-ALGEBRA1` as the next implementation slice. The first exact-linear-algebra pass should be an internal bounded exact rational matrix core using current number-backed `ExactScalar` with strict size/coefficient/denominator growth stops; product-facing `MATRIX-EXACT1`, `POLY-ELIM1`, and bigint-focused `EXACT-SCALAR1` remain later decisions.
- 2026-05-23: `EXACT-LINEAR-ALGEBRA1` establishes exact linear algebra as an internal reusable substrate, not a product Matrix mode or solver feature. The core may support determinant, RREF/rank, square solve, and inverse over capped number-backed exact rationals; product `MATRIX-EXACT1`, symbolic linear-system solving, `POLY-ELIM1`, bigint scalar work, graphing, source-mirror execution, and Labs runner work remain separate future milestones.
- 2026-05-23: `POLY-ELIM1` establishes polynomial elimination only as a bounded internal scalar univariate resultant substrate. Resultants are computed from Sylvester matrices through the exact matrix determinant core; bivariate elimination, Grobner bases, multivariate representation, Equation solver adoption, product Matrix exact mode, graphing, source-mirror execution, copied source, and Labs runner work remain separate future milestones.
- 2026-05-23: Multivariable work is an app-wide variable-semantics roadmap, not a POLY/RAT subtask. Calcwiz must distinguish solve targets, stored numeric variables, symbolic parameters, active variables, and bound variables before `POLY-ELIM2` or product-facing bivariate elimination can proceed; stored variables must never silently override solve targets.
- 2026-05-23: `AREA-MULTIVAR0` selects `VARIABLE-CORE1` as the next implementation milestone. Equation target selection, variable memory, calculus variable widening, polynomial systems, and `POLY-ELIM2` must wait until a shared internal symbol-discovery and variable-role classification core exists.
- 2026-05-23: Variable identifiers are case-sensitive, but coding-style multi-character string variables are deferred. `K` and `k` are distinct symbols; raw adjacent letters such as `hello` should not be silently accepted as one named variable and should instead be treated as multiplied single-character symbols where the parser supports that, or stopped as ambiguous/unsupported until a future explicit named-variable milestone exists.
- 2026-05-23: Reserved-token highlighting is future visible UX work, not part of `VARIABLE-CORE1`. The variable core should classify reserved functions, reserved constants, variables, and ambiguous identifiers so a later `EDITOR-VARIABLE-HINTS1` or `VARIABLE-READBACK1` milestone can color or annotate them without inventing a separate parser policy.
- 2026-05-23: `VARIABLE-CORE1` is internal metadata only. It may expose shared symbol discovery, reserved-name filtering, identifier classification, variable-role facts, and structured stops to internal consumers, but it must not add Equation target UI, variable memory, named string variables, visible semantic highlighting, multivariable solving, `POLY-ELIM2`, graphing, source-mirror execution, or Labs runner behavior.
- 2026-05-23: `EQUATION-TARGET1` is the first visible solve-target foundation, not parameterized solving. Equation may auto-retarget safe single-variable non-`x` equations through the existing `x` backend and show a `Solve for` selector for multi-symbol equations, but multi-symbol parameterized solving, variable memory, named string variables, multivariable solving, `POLY-ELIM2`, graphing, source-mirror execution, and Labs runner work remain separate future milestones.
- 2026-05-24: OOE (Order Of Execution) is accepted as a valid Rust-first kernel execution-order direction, not a new solver or UI controller. The first OOE gates should be `OOE-RS0` readiness/docs and `OOE-RS1` Rust schema plus pure validation. Progressive and atomic solver ideas are future metadata/roadmap work only; do not bundle chunk scheduling, checkpoint ledgers, streaming, cancellation wiring, remote routing, or solver rewrites into the first OOE milestones.
- 2026-05-27: OOE is also the long-term execution traffic-control and observability spine. Future OOE design should include execution priority, host selection, budget enforcement, cancellability, stale-result commit policy, main-thread safety, trace events, diagnostic buffers, and developer/agent observability. The first OOE gates remain conservative (`OOE-RS0`/`OOE-RS1` only); do not bundle runtime routing, solver behavior changes, MCP servers, chunk scheduling, checkpoint ledgers, or real cancellation into the first gates.
- 2026-05-27: `OOE-RS1` adopts typed string newtypes, a small Rust module tree under `src-tauri/src/ooe/`, and structured serde validation errors as the first OOE code foundation. The module may define schema/versioned plan, node, policy, trace-event, and pure validation types, but it must not expose Tauri commands, add a TypeScript bridge, route runtime work, schedule jobs, cancel solvers, change UI behavior, or alter solver outputs.
- 2026-05-27: `OOE-RS2` adopts a Rust-only built-in plan registry with one validated plan per current kernel capability and lightweight descriptors for future command listing. The registry is internal Rust data only; no Tauri commands, TypeScript bridge, runtime routing, scheduling, solver behavior, UI behavior, trace buffer, MCP bridge, or cancellation behavior is introduced.
- 2026-05-27: `OOE-RS3` exposes only narrow Tauri diagnostic commands for listing built-in OOE plan descriptors, fetching a built-in plan, and validating a submitted OOE plan. Invalid plans return a validation report object rather than a command failure; unknown built-in lookups return `null`. RS3 must not add frontend invoke wrappers, route calculator execution, change UI behavior, execute solvers, add trace buffers, introduce MCP diagnostics, or implement scheduler/cancellation behavior.
- 2026-05-27: `OOE-RS4` adds a thin TypeScript diagnostics bridge over the RS3 commands with zod response parsing and explicit unavailable web-preview fallbacks. Rust remains canonical; TypeScript mirrors are adapter convenience only. RS4 must not add UI consumers, runtime routing, solver execution, scheduler/cancellation, trace buffers, history/schema changes, MCP diagnostics, or a TypeScript OOE registry/validator.
- 2026-05-23: `EQUATION-PARAM5` extends selected-target parameterized Equation solving only through bounded exp/log inverse-pair isolation and one safe handoff to existing PARAM1-4 solvers. Supported bases are `e`, common log base `10`, and explicit positive numeric bases not equal to `1`; symbolic bases, log-combine sums/quotients, Lambert W, nested exp/log towers, broad transcendental algebra, variable memory, named string variables, `POLY-ELIM2`, graphing, source-mirror execution, and Labs runner work remain out of scope.
- 2026-05-23: Parameterized Equation solving is now its own `EQUATION-PARAM*` roadmap, not a hidden extension of `EQUATION-TARGET1` or `POLY-ELIM2`. The lane must solve for one explicitly selected target while preserving other symbols as symbolic parameters, family by family: affine/linear first, then bounded polynomial, rational, absolute/radical/power, exponential/logarithmic, and trigonometric slices. This is not variable memory, named string variables, broad transcendental algebra, bivariate elimination, graphing, source-mirror execution, or CAS parity.
- 2026-05-23: `EQUATION-PARAM1` adopts only affine/linear selected-target parameterized solving. Non-target symbols are preserved as symbolic parameters, symbolic coefficients produce explicit nonzero facts, and raw adjacent-letter products such as `xz` remain unsupported unless multiplication is explicit. Polynomial-in-target, rational-target, absolute/radical/power, exponential/logarithmic, and trigonometric parameterized solving remain later `EQUATION-PARAM*` slices.
- 2026-05-23: `EQUATION-PARAM2` adopts only real-guarded quadratic selected-target parameterized solving. Non-target symbols remain symbolic parameters, symbolic leading coefficients produce nonzero facts, discriminants produce real-domain facts, and raw adjacent-letter products remain unsupported unless multiplication is explicit. Higher-degree parameterized polynomial solving, rational target-denominator solving, variable memory, named string variables, bivariate elimination, `POLY-ELIM2`, graphing, source-mirror execution, and Labs runner work remain later milestones.
- 2026-05-23: `EQUATION-PARAM3` adopts only bounded rational selected-target solving by LCD clearing and delegation to existing `EQUATION-PARAM1`/`EQUATION-PARAM2` cleared equations. Original denominator exclusions and simple derived nonzero facts must be preserved. Broad rational simplification, higher-degree cleared equations, nested target denominators, variable memory, named string variables, bivariate elimination, `POLY-ELIM2`, graphing, source-mirror execution, and Labs runner work remain later milestones.
- 2026-05-23: `EQUATION-PARAM4` adopts only bounded nonperiodic selected-target carrier solving. It may isolate absolute-value, square-root, and square-power carriers and delegate generated branch equations to existing `EQUATION-PARAM1`/`EQUATION-PARAM2`/`EQUATION-PARAM3`, but it must not reopen deep or periodic `COMP` composition. Branch/domain facts and delegated denominator facts must be preserved; broader inverse-function composition, variable memory, named string variables, bivariate elimination, `POLY-ELIM2`, graphing, source-mirror execution, and Labs runner work remain later milestones.
- 2026-05-23: Restriction/readback formatting such as `(...)^{-1}` versus `1/(...)` is deferred polish, likely under `EQUATION-PARAM-READBACK1` or `EQUATION-PARAM7`; capability milestones should not expand scope to fix display-only notation unless explicitly planned.
- 2026-05-23: `EQUATION-PARAM6` adopts only direct affine selected-target trigonometric carrier solving. It may isolate one `sin`, `cos`, or `tan` carrier with a target-free affine shell and affine selected-target argument, emit periodic families with integer parameter `n`, honor the active angle unit through explicit inverse-trig conversion factors, and preserve range/nonzero/periodic-family facts. It must not add trig identity solving, multiple-carrier solving, nonlinear trig arguments, periodic/deep `COMP` reopening, variable memory, named string variables, `POLY-ELIM2`, graphing, source-mirror execution, or Labs runner work.
- 2026-05-23: `EQUATION-PARAM7` is readback, Guide, replay, and roadmap polish only. It may normalize selected-target detail wording, improve safe restriction readback, preserve selected targets in history/Guide launches, and add Guide examples for PARAM1 through PARAM6. It must not add solver families, result origins, badges, variable memory, named string variables, `POLY-ELIM2`, graphing, source-mirror execution, or broad CAS behavior.
- 2026-05-23: The post-PARAM7 Equation roadmap reserves `EQUATION-PARAM8` for stronger rational parameterized handling, `EQUATION-PARAM9` for higher-degree/factorable polynomial selected-target solving, `EQUATION-PARAM10` for symbolic-base exp/log policy, `EQUATION-PARAM11` for bounded one-layer composition handoff, and `EQUATION-PARAM12` for mixed-carrier/composition handling. Composition and mixed-carrier work must not be pulled into PARAM7.
- 2026-05-24: `EQUATION-PARAM8` adopts rational selected-target normalization only. It may flatten nested rational structures, rational quotients, parameter-only denominators, and bounded rational sums before LCD clearing, preserve original and easy derived nonzero facts, and return parameter-condition results when the selected target cancels. It must not add higher-degree cleared solving, mixed-carrier/composition solving, symbolic-base exp/log solving, Guide content, variable memory, named string variables, `POLY-ELIM2`, graphing, source-mirror execution, Labs runner work, result origins, or badges.
- 2026-05-24: `EQUATION-PARAM9` adopts factorable selected-target polynomial solving only. It may solve explicit zero-products up to degree 4 by delegating supported linear/quadratic factors to existing selected-target solvers and may reuse the existing exact-rational bounded cubic/quartic factor core. It must not add general symbolic cubic/quartic formulas, degree greater than 4, partial solution sets from unsupported factors, Guide content, rational widening, composition, symbolic-base exp/log solving, variable memory, named string variables, `POLY-ELIM2`, graphing, source-mirror execution, Labs runner work, result origins, or badges.

- 2026-05-25: `EQUATION-ISOLATION1` adopts one-island selected-target isolation only. It must not add two-island algebra, broad simplification, Lambert W, symbolic cube-root/power isolation, Equation symbolic stored-value substitution, graphing, `POLY-ELIM2`, source-mirror work, Labs runner work, result-origin changes, or history-schema changes.
- 2026-05-25: `EDITOR-PERF1` adopts an analysis-boundary model rather than parent input debouncing. MathLive editor values and Run/EXE execution stay current, while preview rendering, variable hints, Equation target discovery, and algebra-transform eligibility may be debounced, huge-input guarded, and locally error-contained with last-safe output preservation. Solver cancellation, job infrastructure, and OOE wiring remain deferred.
- 2026-05-25: `EDITOR-RUNTIME1` makes display-header `Run`, `Stop`, and `Restart Editor` controls operate only over the editor-analysis boundary. `Stop` cancels queued/deferred editor analysis and preserves last-safe output; it must not claim solver cancellation or job interruption. `Run` still uses the current draft through the existing EXE/F1 execution path, while `Restart Editor` clears the active editor draft and stale editor-analysis cards before restarting analysis. True job/cancellation infrastructure remains deferred to OOE.
- 2026-05-25: App editor/input contrast is surface-aware: dark workspace editors, hint chips, and form controls use editor contrast tokens, while LCD display panels keep LCD ink. Equation Home must provide Back/F5/Esc escape parity to the launcher instead of trapping the user in the menu.
- 2026-05-25: Result cards use shared display hierarchy: exact output is labeled `Answer`, supplement restrictions are labeled `Valid when` and stay visible by default, and solver facts/method/context remain below as details. - 2026-05-25: `APP-MEMORY1` adopts autosaved calculator memory rather than manual save prompts. - 2026-05-27: `APP-MEMORY1` calculator memory is core-only, not session restore. - 2026-05-27: Future solver execution modes are `classic`, `progressive`, and `atomic`. `classic` is the current one-shot calculator/CAS-style solve UX and may later use local compute or one external SSH/compute source. `progressive` is chunked asynchronous execution with a governor, search-first materialization, output streaming, and idempotent checkpoint/resume through a lightweight ledger plus persistent output storage; it may also use local compute or one external source. `atomic` is not the opposite of progressive and is not one-shot: it uses the same chunked/checkpointable/search-first architecture as progressive, but with a more aggressive resource policy and the ability to fan out across multiple external SSH/compute hosts. `OOE-RS11` should be reframed as solver execution policy metadata only; it must not implement chunk scheduling, checkpoint ledgers, streaming output, resumability, cancellation wiring, remote execution, or solver rewrites.
- 2026-05-27: Atomic solver mode is deferred for now because the aggressive resource policy and multi-external-host SSH/compute fanout make it harder than Classic or Progressive. Near-term OOE/PSE planning should actively handle only `classic` and `progressive`; `atomic` may remain in metadata as a future reserved mode but must not be implemented, exposed, or used to drive near-term execution behavior until Progressive is proven locally and the external/multi-host compute boundary is deliberately reopened.
- 2026-05-27: `OOE-RS11` adds only Classic/Progressive solver execution policy metadata to the Rust-owned OOE node schema. Current built-in capabilities remain `classic`, unchunked, final-only, full-materialization, local, and normal-resource. `progressive` metadata may validate only when chunked and remains future readiness; `singleExternal` is metadata-only and does not implement remote execution. `atomic`, multi-external-host fanout, aggressive resource policy, scheduler/cancellation wiring, checkpoint ledgers, streaming renderers, resumability, MCP diagnostics, UI controls, and Rust solver execution remain out of scope.
- 2026-05-27: `OOE-RS12` adds only job identity, input revision, and stale-commit assessment contract/helpers. `AlwaysCommit` commits unconditionally; `CommitLatestOnly` commits only matching input revisions and otherwise stale-drops; `CommitIfCurrent` commits matching revisions, stale-drops mismatches, and skips when no active revision exists; no-job routes are marked not-applicable. Current Equation, Calculate, and Table pilots/controllers must not enforce this contract yet. RS12 is a prerequisite for later stale-result gating, cancellation, editor containment, and Progressive Solver work, but it must not add runtime routing, scheduler behavior, cancellation, UI, history/result schema changes, trace buffers, MCP diagnostics, remote execution, or Rust solver execution.

Runtime controllers/hooks must still commit only payloads. RS13 must not add stale-result enforcement, cancellation, scheduling, UI, history/result schema changes, solver behavior changes, Rust execution, trace buffers, MCP diagnostics, remote execution, or Progressive Solver implementation.

OOE bridge/preflight failures remain fail-open when revisions still match.
