- 2026-05-29: `OOE-RS24` is the first cooperative OOE traffic-control pilot and is limited to active Table builds. Table remains TypeScript-executed on the main thread, but the OOE wrapper may generate rows cooperatively, yield between row batches, check the active job registry for cancellation requests, and stop with a controlled visible cancellation note. Cancelled Table jobs are terminal `cancelled` in registry/diagnostics, use `notApplicable` commit decision metadata, do not replace the previous `TableResponse`, and do not clear replay substitution snapshots. Synchronous `runTableMode` remains behavior-stable for direct callers. `OOE-RS25` remains the first isolated runtime pilot. Equation cancellation is explicitly post-RS25 expansion work, not part of RS24 or a redefinition of RS25.
- 2026-05-29: `OOE-RS25` is the first isolated runtime pilot and is limited to active Table builds. The active `table.build` OOE plan now targets `table-worker-runtime`, a Web Worker host with isolated budget policy, hard-stop cancellation, and worker-safe thread-safety metadata. `table-runtime` remains the cooperative main-thread fallback. The Table wrapper attempts the worker first, falls back when the worker is unavailable or fails, terminates the worker on cancellation, and records host execution metadata in OOE diagnostics without storing table rows. Completed payloads must match `runTableMode`; stale drops and cancelled visible-state rules remain unchanged. RS25 does not add Equation cancellation, Progressive Solver behavior, Rust solver execution, public diagnostics UI, MCP endpoint, history/result schema changes, row-limit changes, or Table math semantic changes.
- 2026-05-29: After `OOE-RS25`, the OOE roadmap is intentionally paused before deeper Equation cancellation/isolation. The post-RS25 OOE upgrade notes remain valid and must not be renamed: `OOE-RS26` is Equation guarded-stage cancellation checkpoints, `OOE-RS27` is an Equation heavy-helper isolation pilot, and `OOE-RS28` broadens Equation cancellation coverage. The pause exists because inequalities and complex-number foundations will expand solver semantics, domain facts, answer modes, and provenance; those foundations should be established before OOE wraps more Equation internals.
- 2026-05-29: Inequalities and complex support should be planned together under `.memory/research/roadmaps/inequalities-complex-foundation-roadmap.md`. The preferred strategy is simultaneous but coordinated: first define shared domain/value/fact contracts, then advance bounded inequalities and complex-number rails in parallel. Do not let the inequality rail and complex rail independently redefine "real solution", "no real solution", complex roots, validity conditions, or stored-value policy.
- 2026-06-02: Complex support should be opt-in through a top-header `Complex`/complex-domain toggle rather than silently appearing in ordinary real-first workflows. When disabled, users should continue seeing real-domain behavior and "no real solution" style outcomes where appropriate; when enabled, bounded complex-capable routes may return visibly marked complex-domain answers. Inequality and complex foundations must be reusable cores, not one-off Equation hacks, and should reuse existing branch/domain/assumptions cores where relevant for branch facts, principal/range facts, denominator exclusions, radicand requirements, and validity readback instead of inventing parallel fact systems.
- 2026-06-02: Inequality and complex product adoption is Equation-first until the semantics stabilize. Shared value/domain/fact, branch, interval, and complex-number cores should be reusable across the app, but visible solver expansion into Calculate, Table, Calculus, Advanced Calc, Trigonometry, Geometry, Statistics, Matrix, or Vector is deferred. The first product-facing complex and inequality routes should live in Equation so answer modes, selected targets, validity facts, and "no real solution" versus complex-enabled behavior can be hardened in one workspace before wider adoption.
- 2026-06-02: `INEQ-COMPLEX-FOUNDATION0` freezes inequality/complex readiness as a documentation-only audit. The shared answer-domain vocabulary is `real`, `complex`, `conditional-real`, and `unknown-domain`; solution kind remains separate as exact symbolic, approximate numeric, isolate/rearranged formula, inequality solution set, or condition/fact-only stop. `VALUE-DOMAIN-CORE1` is the next implementation substrate and should extend/reuse `assumptions-core`, `branch-core`, `domain-range-core`, and the existing `numeric/complex` primitive instead of adding visible solver behavior directly.
- 2026-06-02: `VALUE-DOMAIN-CORE1` implements the shared internal TypeScript value/domain/fact contract only. It extends the existing assumption fact spine with inequality and complex-domain fact vocabulary and adds value-domain metadata builders, but deliberately does not thread metadata into `DisplayOutcome`, add the top-header `Complex` toggle, change OOE diagnostics, alter history/app-state/Rust schemas, or expose inequality/complex solving. `COMPLEX-CORE1` and `INEQUALITY-CORE1` remain separate future cores that must consume this shared contract.
- 2026-06-02: `COMPLEX-CORE1` implements only the bounded internal complex-number primitive and readback helpers. It adds complex conjugate, argument, polar construction, integer powers, principal nth roots, deterministic all nth roots, and principal/all-branches readback helpers under `src/lib/numeric/complex.ts`. It deliberately does not add a complex parser, the top-header `Complex` toggle, Equation adoption, stored complex variables, complex Approximate search, `DisplayOutcome`, history, app-state, OOE, Rust, Tauri schema, or visible UI behavior changes.
- 2026-06-02: `INEQUALITY-CORE1` implements only the bounded internal inequality interval/fact core. It adds typed one-variable real interval unions, typed constructors, canonical finite-union normalization, intersection, containment, empty-set detection, equality, and readback helpers under `src/lib/algebra/inequality-core.ts`, plus conversion into `inequality-core` assumption facts and `ValueDomainMetadata`. It deliberately does not add inequality parsing, Equation adoption, broad nonlinear solving, piecewise/graphing behavior, public assumptions UI, `DisplayOutcome`, history, app-state, OOE, Rust, Tauri schema, or visible UI behavior changes.
- 2026-06-02: `EQUATION-DOMAIN-INTENT1` adds only the product-facing complex-domain intent toggle and request/provenance threading. The top header exposes persisted `Complex Off` / `Complex On`, stored as `settings.equationDomainIntent` with `real` default and desktop/web sanitization. Equation symbolic requests, OOE input revisions, active request refs, history replay, and rich OOE provenance now carry the intent, and Complex-enabled Equation results show a visible `Domain intent: Complex` badge. Solvers remain real-first; no complex answers, complex parser, stored complex values, inequality solving, non-Equation adoption, history schema change beyond the optional history field, OOE behavior change, Rust solver execution, or result semantics change is included. `COMPLEX-EQUATION1` remains the first milestone allowed to return bounded complex Equation answers.
- 2026-06-02: `COMPLEX-EQUATION1` is the first product-facing complex Equation adoption slice. Complex answers are opt-in and limited to `Complex On` plus Equation answer mode `Exact`; bounded quadratics with negative discriminants and simple selected-target power equations may return marked complex-domain branches. `Complex Off` keeps real-first stops and successes, `Approximate` stays real numeric interval solving only, and `Isolate` stays textbook rearrangement. Optional `answerDomain` metadata may appear on outcomes/history and OOE provenance. The milestone deliberately does not add a complex parser, stored complex variables, complex Approximate search, inequality solving, non-Equation adoption, broad transcendental complex solving, OOE runtime behavior changes, or Rust solver execution.
- 2026-06-03: `INEQUALITY-EQUATION1` is the first product-facing inequality adoption slice and is Equation-only. It supports only top-level ordered relations `<`, `<=`, `>`, and `>=` in Equation answer mode `Exact` when they reduce to one-variable numeric-coefficient linear inequalities `a*x+b relation 0`; negative coefficients flip the direction, and constant true/false reductions may return all-real or empty-set results. Results use `INEQUALITY-CORE1`, `answerDomain: conditional-real`, and `solutionKind: inequality-solution-set`; history/replay and Equation OOE provenance may carry `solutionKind`. `Approximate` and `Isolate` return controlled inequality guidance, `Complex On` adds a real-order note without changing inequality math, and `!=`, symbolic-parameter, multivariable, quadratic, rational sign-chart, absolute-value, trig/log/exp, chained, non-Equation, broad parser, stored-value policy, OOE behavior, and Rust solver expansion remain out of scope.
- 2026-06-03: `ANSWER-DOMAIN-READBACK1` is readback/layout polish only. It fixes persisted History bootstrap scanability by making collapsed cards non-shrinking input previews and expanded cards contained readbacks; it also consolidates Equation domain/solution chips, keeps ordered-inequality Complex intent notes distinct from actual complex-domain badges, fixes prose readback for ASCII inequality operators such as `<=`, and normalizes simple bounded complex branch display where safe. It must not add solver capability, parsers, stored complex values, history schema changes, OOE runtime behavior changes, or non-Equation inequality/complex adoption.
- 2026-06-03: After `ANSWER-DOMAIN-READBACK1`, the next larger inequality/complex leap should not use the vague milestone name `EQUATION-ADVANCED-DOMAIN1`. Keep the shared-substrate idea as `POLYNOMIAL-DOMAIN-CORE1` only if it stays narrow: classifier/extractor/fact metadata for one-variable polynomial/rational shapes, degrees, coefficients/factors, and domain exclusions, with no solving or visible behavior by itself. Product-facing expansion should remain explicit and separate: `INEQUALITY-EQUATION2` for bounded quadratic/factored real inequality sets and `COMPLEX-EQUATION2` for broader bounded polynomial/power complex answers.
- 2026-06-03: `POLYNOMIAL-DOMAIN-CORE1` implements only a shared pure-core classifier over existing exact polynomial and rational-function primitives. It may classify one-variable polynomial/rational metadata, expose degree/shape/coefficient/domain-exclusion facts, add `polynomial-domain-core` as an assumption fact source, and provide value-domain metadata helpers for future consumers. It must not solve, change Equation routes, change OOE, alter history/UI/result schemas, or implement `INEQUALITY-EQUATION2` / `COMPLEX-EQUATION2`.
- 2026-06-03: `INEQUALITY-EQUATION2` and `COMPLEX-EQUATION2` were implemented in one coordinated session but remain separate product rails. `INEQUALITY-EQUATION2` owns real ordered polynomial inequality solution sets in Equation `Exact`; `COMPLEX-EQUATION2` owns opt-in complex exact branches in Equation `Exact + Complex On`. They may share the Equation polynomial-domain helper, but they must not merge contracts, answer-mode wording, or future scope. Ordered inequalities remain real-domain-only even when Complex intent is enabled.
- 2026-06-03: `INEQUALITY-EQUATION2` supports one-variable numeric-coefficient polynomial inequalities up to degree 4 only when exact real roots are available through the bounded path. It may use sign charts over exact roots and return finite interval unions via `INEQUALITY-CORE1`. It must not add rational sign charts, symbolic-parameter inequality solving, multivariable inequality solving, chained inequalities, `!=`, Approximate inequality sampling, Isolate inequality rearrangement, non-Equation adoption, OOE behavior changes, or Rust solver execution.
- 2026-06-03: `COMPLEX-EQUATION2` supports factorable one-variable polynomial equations up to degree 4 only when `Complex On` and `Exact` are selected, factorization reduces to linear/quadratic factors, and at least one branch is non-real. It must not add a complex parser, stored complex values, complex Approximate search, Isolate complex solving, unfactorable cubic/quartic formula expansion, numeric-only fake exact roots, non-Equation adoption, OOE behavior changes, or Rust solver execution.
- 2026-06-03: `INEQUALITY-EQUATION3` is the major Equation-only guarded real inequality engine. It may use exact roots/critical points plus numeric sign-cell sampling for proof classification, but numeric-only roots must not become Exact answers. It may cover factorable rationals, textbook abs/radical forms, monotone log/exp forms, bounded supported composition, and direct affine periodic trig inequalities in Equation `Exact`; it must keep `Approximate` and `Isolate` as guidance-only for inequality inputs, keep ordered inequalities real-domain-only under `Complex On`, and avoid non-Equation adoption, graphing, chained inequalities, symbolic-parameter/multivariable solving, OOE behavior changes, or Rust solver execution.
- 2026-06-03: `INEQUALITY-READBACK-COMPOSITION1` moves inequality validity restrictions into the existing `Valid when` card and raises guarded composition without changing answer-mode boundaries. Main answers remain solution sets only; denominator exclusions, radicand/log domains, monotone-base facts, tangent singularities, period/family facts, and real-order notes belong in `exactSupplementLatex`, not duplicated proof details. Finite guarded wrappers may compose up to 4 layers. Trig composition supports representable two-trig-layer cases where an outer `sin`/`cos`/`tan` reduces to an inner affine `sin`/`cos` range; inner `tan` support is limited to all/empty outer-range cases with tangent singularities in `Valid when`, while nontrivial tangent-branch subfamilies remain guarded stops until the periodic-set model can represent them honestly. Ordered inequalities stay real-domain-only even under `Complex On`.
- 2026-06-03: Inequality wrapper polish for `INEQUALITY-READBACK-COMPOSITION1` is narrow shell isolation, not a broad rearrangement engine. Exact numeric target-free additive/multiplicative shells around a supported inequality carrier may be peeled before routing, including direction flips for negative numeric factors; symbolic factor signs and deeper algebra still stop. Verbose `Valid when` and proof/detail result blocks may collapse/expand like History cards, but without deletion and without hiding the main `Answer`.
- 2026-06-03: `INEQUALITY-PREIMAGE-READBACK2` completes the pending inequality preimage/readback work as a single combined milestone with `INEQUALITY-PREIMAGE1`. Abs-affine periodic inequalities may flatten through `|ax+b|` into `x`-alone branch families when the split into `ax+b` and `-(ax+b)` is safe; distance-family notation remains proof/fallback only. Periodic readback should prefer calculator-style symbolic shifts (`k\pi`, `2k\pi`, `\frac{k\pi}{5}`) and keep tangent singularities, branch-index facts, period facts, and real-order notes in `Valid when`. The milestone may respect `EXACT` / `DECIMAL` / `BOTH` output style for inverse-trig thresholds, and may extend finite rational/nested preimages through the existing bounded helper. It must still stop periodic-over-rational, periodic-over-nonlinear-abs, symbolic-threshold, chained, multivariable, and Tier 4 nonlinear periodic cases.
- 2026-06-03: `INEQUALITY-STABILITY1` is an Equation-only audit/fix stability gate after `INEQUALITY-PREIMAGE-READBACK2`, not a capability expansion. It may harden relation operator normalization for typed, pasted, copied, and replayed inequalities; add regression coverage for already-supported polynomial, rational, abs/radical/log/exp, finite composition, trig, and preimage families; and polish guidance/readback only where defects are exposed. It must not add new inequality families, Approximate inequality sampling, Isolate inequality solving, graphing, chained/multivariable/symbolic-parameter solving, complex ordered inequalities, non-Equation adoption, OOE behavior changes, or Rust solver execution.
- 2026-06-04: `COMPLEX-EQUATION3` is the major algebraic complex Equation milestone after `COMPLEX-INPUT1`. It may solve bounded algebraic `Exact + Complex On` routes with explicit imaginary constants, factorable one-variable polynomial equations through degree 4 when branches are exact/readable, selected-target power carriers already covered by the bounded complex route, and supported rational equations by numerator roots with denominator exclusions. It must not add stored complex values, non-Equation adoption, Approximate complex search, Isolate complex solving, complex trig/log/exp solving, Cardano/Ferrari unfactorable cubic/quartic formulas, numeric-only fake exact roots, OOE behavior changes, or Rust solver execution.
- 2026-06-04: `COMPLEX-READBACK-STABILITY1` is stabilization/readback polish only. Equation analysis treats `i` and `\imaginaryI` as reserved imaginary units that must display as `i reserved unit` and must not appear as solve targets, symbolic parameters, stored ignored values, or ambiguity hints. `j` and `k` remain ordinary symbols. Awkward exact complex branch construction may read back with clean polar/cis notation, while simple rectangular complex values should stay rectangular. The milestone must not add a new complex solver family, complex trig/log/exp solving, stored complex values, complex Approximate search, Isolate complex solving, reserved-symbol override syntax, non-Equation adoption, OOE behavior changes, or Rust solver execution.
- 2026-06-04: `COMPLEX-DISPLAY-SETTINGS1` is readback/settings stabilization only. Persist `complexExactForm` as `rectangular`, `polar`, or `cis`, defaulting to `rectangular`; do not include an `auto` mode. `rectangular` uses exact rectangular branches, `polar` uses expanded `r(cos(theta)+i sin(theta))`, and `cis` uses compact `r cis(theta)`. The top-header `Complex On/Off` button remains only the domain-intent toggle. `ImaginaryUnit` / `\imaginaryI` must display as the imaginary unit, never numeric `1`. No new complex solver family, complex Approximate search, Isolate complex solving, stored complex values, reserved-symbol override syntax, non-Equation adoption, OOE runtime behavior change, or Rust solver execution is included.
- 2026-06-04: `COMPLEX-PREIMAGE-EQUATION1` is the guarded complex function-preimage milestone after `COMPLEX-DISPLAY-SETTINGS1`. In Equation `Exact + Complex On`, it may reduce supported `ln` / `log`, `exp`, direct `sin`/`cos`/`tan`, affine shells, rational-linear inners, bounded powers, and selected quadratic-over-linear rational equations into exact inner equations, using principal complex log plus explicit integer-periodic families where needed. Periodic-power answers should use concise root-family main readback with expanded branches in detail sections. It must not add complex Approximate, complex Isolate, stored complex values, non-Equation adoption, absolute-value complex locus solving, broad unfactorable cubic/quartic formulas, OOE behavior changes, or Rust solver execution.
- 2026-06-04: `COMPLEX-PREIMAGE-EQUATION2` is the broader rational and true two-trig-layer complex preimage milestone before the paired complex preimage readback/stability pass. In Equation `Exact + Complex On`, it may clear supported rational denominators when the resulting exact equation stays inside bounded linear/quadratic/polynomial/power routes, and it may solve outer `sin`/`cos`/`tan` over inner `sin`/`cos`/`tan` with independent integer branch parameters such as `k,n in Z`. Inner trig arguments may be affine or bounded selected-target powers through degree 4. It must still stop periodic-over-rational, degree > 4, multivariable, absolute-value complex locus, complex Approximate, complex Isolate, stored complex values, non-Equation adoption, OOE behavior changes, and Rust solver execution.
- 2026-06-04: `DISPLAY-DETAIL-MATH-RENDER1` is the app-wide detail-card render contract paired with `COMPLEX-PREIMAGE-READBACK1` and `COMPLEX-PREIMAGE-STABILITY1`. `DisplayDetailSection.lines: string[]` remains the compatibility surface, while route producers may add whole-line math metadata or mixed prose/math parts. The display layer should render explicit math through `MathStatic`, keep ordinary prose through `NotationText`, and only apply conservative known-route prose splitting; it must not force arbitrary prose through math parsing. This pass is readback/stability only and must not add new complex solving capability, complex Approximate, complex Isolate, stored complex values, OOE behavior changes, non-Equation adoption, or Rust solver execution.
- 2026-06-05: `OOE-RS26` resumes the paused OOE roadmap as Equation guarded-stage cancellation checkpoints only. Stop requests for active `equation.solve` jobs may be observed at guarded-stage boundaries, after no-outcome stage exits, before recursive guarded handoffs, and before direct symbolic fallback. Cancelled Equation envelopes must be terminal `cancelled` with `notApplicable` commit assessment, preserve previous visible result/history/replay substitutions, and show only transient stopped status. This milestone must not interrupt a currently executing heavy helper mid-call, migrate Equation to a worker, execute solvers in Rust, add a scheduler rewrite, expose public diagnostics UI/MCP, change result/history schemas, or add solver capability.
- 2026-06-05: `OOE-RS27` is the first Equation heavy-helper isolation pilot and is scoped only to the terminal guarded `direct-symbolic` helper. The `equation.solve` OOE plan remains hosted by `equation-runtime`; `equation-direct-symbolic-worker-runtime` is recorded as helper-level host evidence, not as a route migration. Worker completion must preserve main-thread direct-symbolic payload parity; worker unavailable/init/runtime failure may fallback to the main-thread helper with evidence; Stop cancellation must hard-terminate the worker and must not fallback. RS27 must not migrate the full Equation solver, interrupt other helper families, execute solvers in Rust, expose public diagnostics UI/MCP, change result/history schemas, or add solver capability.
- 2026-06-05: `OOE-RS28` broadens Equation cancellation coverage cooperatively inside the guarded async path. The Equation-local control contract may adapt OOE `yieldIfBudgetExceeded`, and helper evidence may include helper/family/branch/candidate metadata. RS28 may add async checkpoint/yield coverage for guarded stages, recursive guarded handoffs, substitution branches, and candidate-validation preparation, but it must preserve RS26 cancelled visible-state behavior and RS27 direct-symbolic worker isolation. RS28 must not add solver capability, migrate the full Equation solver to a worker, add another isolated host, execute solvers in Rust, expose public diagnostics UI/MCP, or change result/history schemas.
- 2026-06-05: `OOE-RS29` chooses the in-app developer diagnostics inspector path, not MCP. The inspector is dev-gated by `VITE_SHOW_OOE_DIAGNOSTICS === '1'`, is read-only over diagnostics except for clearing in-memory diagnostics/recent-job buffers and copying one selected record as JSON, and must not add public user UI, persistence, export files, Tauri diagnostics commands, MCP endpoints, solver behavior changes, scheduler changes, result/history schema changes, or full result/table-row storage.
- 2026-06-06: `OOE-RS30` makes `equation.solve` the first full Equation worker runtime-shell pilot and adds transient History launch-order tickets. The built-in Equation plan may prefer `equation-worker-runtime` with `equation-runtime` as init/unavailable fallback only; worker cancellation hard-terminates and preserves RS26/RS28 no-commit visible-state behavior. Pending History tickets reserve launch-order positions and may expose Stop while running, but only finalized completed entries persist an optional launch-order key. RS30 must not add solver capability, non-Equation worker migration, scheduler rewrite, public diagnostics expansion, Rust solver execution, persisted fake pending records, or retry on main thread after a worker runtime failure has started.
- 2026-06-06: `OOE-RS31` locks the widening strategy as one central runtime-shell contract with multiple per-workspace shells, and keeps that contract separate from launch-ticket History ordering. Equation and Table are the only RS31 implementation lanes: Equation keeps RS30 behavior after extraction, while Table adopts pending History tickets and active Computing/Stopping status. Calculate, editor analysis, Advanced Calc, Trigonometry, Statistics, Geometry, Matrix, and Vector are audited and deferred rather than migrated blindly. RS31 must not add new solver capability, non-Equation/Table worker migration, scheduler rewrite, Rust solver execution, public diagnostics expansion, result schema changes, or persisted fake pending records.
- 2026-06-10: `CALCULATE-BOUNDARY0` locks Calculate as Calcwiz's quickform evaluator, not a guided topic workbench. Calculate may call shared algebra, calculus, trigonometry, numeric, domain, and readback capabilities for compact one-shot answers and transformations, including essential validity facts such as denominator exclusions. Rich guided derivatives, integrals, limits, periodic/composition analysis, method panels, proof trees, and future step-by-step flows belong to topic workspaces or reusable step capabilities. Because Calculate still has visible guided calculus/workbench remnants, do `CALCULATE-SURFACE1` and `CALCULATE-RESTRICTIONS1` before `CALCULATE-RUNTIME-SHELL1`; do not widen Calculate OOE/tickets while its product boundary is still blurry.
- 2026-06-10: `CALCULATE-RESTRICTIONS1` preserves validity facts already emitted by Calculate transform cores, such as denominator exclusions after removable-factor cancellation, through the existing `Valid when` path. It does not add a broad domain analyzer, guided proof tree, step-by-step engine, OOE runtime shell, or launch-ticket behavior.
- 2026-06-11: Branch-heavy finite answers should render as separate row-level math islands, not one giant horizontal math object and not a count-only receipt. Display may split a safe branch row into a prefix island (`target relation`) plus a branch-expression island for responsiveness, but the full row LaTeX and full `exactLatex` remain available for accessibility, tests, Copy Result, To Editor, history, replay, and stored output. This remains a Display responsibility; OOE still owns only compute traffic control, commit/drop legality, cancellation, diagnostics, and history-ticket ordering.
- 2026-06-11: OOE consistency fixes should use existing OOE systems only. Do not introduce an event bus, Surface Protocol, Supercarrier compartments, public SDK, plugin architecture, or remote-compute protocol as part of host/plan cleanup. Host descriptors are the source of truth for plan-node cancellation policy, thread-safety, and default result stability; compatibility workspace pilots should mirror active worker-primary hosts so diagnostics/provenance do not drift.
- 2026-06-12: `DISPLAY-PRODUCER-METADATA3` widens only producer-owned finite branch metadata where the producer already has real branch arrays before formatting. Display should prefer validated `branchReadback`, fall back to safe extraction, and fail closed to the original math block for ambiguous output. Full `exactLatex` remains canonical for copy, editor, history, replay, and storage. Do not add branch metadata to facts, grids, tables, tuple systems, inequalities, periodic families, or ordinary measurement summaries.
- 2026-06-12: `DISPLAY-SCHEDULER-POLISH1` keeps committed-result render scheduling in Display, not OOE. Answer/error content should mount first, `Valid when` second, then approx/warnings, periodic-family blocks, and details. Later blocks may mount one display turn/frame at a time, collapsed math-heavy bodies may lazy-mount only when opened, and the display status may show `Rendering result` while committed blocks remain queued. OOE still owns launch, cancellation, stale gates, commit/drop, diagnostics, and history tickets.
- 2026-06-12: `FILE-SIZE-RATCHET1` adds an enforced file-size ratchet to `test:gate`: tracked `src/**/*.ts|tsx` files are capped at 900 lines by default, pre-existing larger files are capped at their introduction size +5% in `tools/file-size-baseline.json`, and `--update-baseline` may only lower or remove caps. Raising any cap requires a deliberate, review-visible edit to the baseline file with justification. Growth past a cap should be answered by extraction, not appending; this guards the post-APPMAIN-SLIM shape against regrowth.
- 2026-06-12: `MEMORY-STATE-SLIM1` fixes `.memory/current-state.md` as a bounded current operating snapshot: at most 500 lines, enforced by the memory-protocol validator. Finished-milestone history moves verbatim to `.memory/research/milestones/` archive files instead of accumulating in current-state. Future posture updates should replace or trim existing bullets rather than append history; the journal, sessions, and decisions files remain the chronological record.
- 2026-06-12: Agent workflow guardrail decision: meaningful code/tooling/UX/workflow commits must include durable memory updates or an explicit no-memory-needed note; internal milestone slices are verification gates/checkpoints rather than automatic `MILESTONE1a/b/c` commit boundaries unless the user requests split commits; file-size ratchet caps must lower to exact current line counts after slimming with no percentage headroom.
- 2026-06-12: Repo structure reorganization decision: the next organization lane starts with `REPO-STRUCTURE-AUDIT1`, followed by scoped AppMain extraction and move-only district splits for Equation/Algebra before any cleanup/deletion. Structure work must preserve solver behavior, OOE behavior, display policy, schemas, worker hosts, capability IDs, history/replay compatibility, and current product boundaries unless a later named milestone explicitly changes one of those contracts.
- 2026-06-12: `REPO-STRUCTURE-AUDIT1` locks the first recommended source move as `EQUATION-DISTRICT-SPLIT1` for the `equation-parameterized-*` selected-target family. The split should be move-only, keep paired tests beside implementation, preserve exports/behavior/history/OOE/display contracts, and avoid splitting `composition-stage.ts` in the same slice. `APPMAIN-SLIM6` remains the next orchestration-risk slice, but Equation district visibility is the immediate Explorer/navigation pain.
- 2026-06-12: `EQUATION-DISTRICT-SPLIT1` completed the move-only selected-target parameterized district. The `equation-parameterized-*` implementation/test family now lives under `src/lib/equation/parameterized/` with short filenames, while `equation-selected-target-isolation.ts` stays at the Equation root as the dispatcher/seam. Preserve emitted runtime/source strings like `equation-parameterized-trig`; path-migrate file-size ratchet entries manually for moved over-cap files because `--update-baseline` lowers/removes existing caps but does not add new moved-path caps automatically.
- 2026-06-12: `APPMAIN-SLIM6` completed the Trigonometry-only hook extraction. `src/app/runtime/useTrigonometryRuntime.ts` owns Trig screen/menu state, draft state, refs, Guide seed/example loading, history replay restoration, current/full reset helpers, and `runTrigAction` while preserving `trigonometry.evaluate`, worker/fallback host IDs, launch-ticket/stale-gate behavior, history/replay payloads, schemas, and Trigonometry workspace props. `AppMain` remains the cross-mode orchestration root; do not fold Equation districts, Algebra moves, or a global reducer/event bus into this slice.
- 2026-06-13: `APPMAIN-SLIM7` completed the Statistics-only hook extraction. `src/app/runtime/useStatisticsRuntime.ts` owns Statistics screen/menu state, dataset/frequency/source-sync state, probability/inference/point-set state, draft state, refs, Guide example loading, history replay restoration, current/full reset helpers, and `runStatisticsAction` while preserving `statistics.evaluate`, worker/fallback host IDs, launch-ticket/stale-gate behavior, history/replay payloads, schemas, and Statistics workspace props. `AppMain` remains the cross-mode orchestration root; continue `APPMAIN-SLIM8` as Geometry-only rather than a global reducer/event-bus slice.
